People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess I came in because… nothing really feels like mine anymore, if that makes sense. Like, the things I used to care about, the things that made me feel like I was me—I just don’t feel anything for them now.

I used to get excited about murals, you know, seeing them around town or thinking about colors and lines and what someone was trying to say with them. I used to run, and even if I wasn’t fast or anything, there was a feeling to it. Basketball too—watching it, playing a little, talking about it. And even at work, making a really good drink, getting it just right, that used to give me some pride.

Now I just kind of stand there. I look at the same stuff and it’s like… nothing. Like I’m watching someone else’s life from across the room. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me enough to come in.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it’s more like… I don’t even really want to do them anymore, not in the way I used to. Or maybe I go through the motion of wanting to, because I remember that I used to be that person. Like with murals, I’ll see one and I know, in my head, that normally I’d stop and look at the details, the colors, the way the lines are done, all of that. I used to get this little spark from it. Now I just look at it and it’s flat. Like it has nothing to do with me.

Same with running or basketball. It’s not even that I’m sitting there wishing I could enjoy it and then failing. It’s more like the wanting part got shut off too. And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. It doesn’t land anywhere.

So I guess the honest answer is both, but mostly I’ve lost interest. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.

I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. Like… I don’t walk around thinking, “I’m sad,” exactly. It’s not always that clear. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.

I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.

It’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.

I’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.

So I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.

Most days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”

Sometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.

As far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.

I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.

And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. It doesn’t land anywhere.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is both, but mostly I’ve lost interest. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or depressed most of the time, even outside of not feeling interested in those activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. Like… I don’t walk around thinking, “I’m sad,” exactly. It’s not always that clear. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.\n\nBut if you mean, do I feel low even when I’m not specifically thinking about murals or running or work or whatever… yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worse about yourself than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.\n\nI’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.\n\nSo I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.\n\nI mean, at work there are moments where I feel like I’m dragging, like everything takes more effort than it should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. If it’s happening, I’m not very aware of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I have some clear thing to tell you, like a plan or anything I’ve decided. It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.

I guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I haven’t really sat there and made a decision about it. It’s more like these thoughts pass through, and I try not to look straight at them too long.

So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah. That part I have noticed, pretty strongly.

It’s not just being tired at the end of the night, because I’m used to being tired after a shift. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. By the time I carry a couple cases of liquor or set up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double, even if I barely did anything earlier that day.

And those aren’t even huge tasks, you know? They’re things I’ve done a thousand times without thinking. Stocking bottles, cutting fruit, dragging a trash bag out, wiping everything down before we open. Lately it feels like every step has a weight tied to it. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.

It’s embarrassing, honestly. I keep telling myself, “Come on, just do the next thing,” but even small stuff feels like I have to talk myself into it. Not because I don’t know how to do it, just because there’s no energy there. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.

Or maybe I go through the motion of wanting to, because I remember that I used to be that person. Like with murals, I’ll see one and I know, in my head, that normally I’d stop and look at the details, the colors, the way the lines are done, all of that. I used to get this little spark from it. Now I just look at it and it’s flat. Like it has nothing to do with me.\n\nSame with running or basketball. It’s not even that I’m sitting there wishing I could enjoy it and then failing. It’s more like the wanting part got shut off too. And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. It doesn’t land anywhere.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is both, but mostly I’ve lost interest. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or depressed most of the time, even outside of not feeling interested in those activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. Like… I don’t walk around thinking, “I’m sad,” exactly. It’s not always that clear. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.\n\nBut if you mean, do I feel low even when I’m not specifically thinking about murals or running or work or whatever… yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worse about yourself than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.\n\nI’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.\n\nSo I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.\n\nI mean, at work there are moments where I feel like I’m dragging, like everything takes more effort than it should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. If it’s happening, I’m not very aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live, or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I have some clear thing to tell you, like a plan or anything I’ve decided. It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I haven’t really sat there and made a decision about it. It’s more like these thoughts pass through, and I try not to look straight at them too long.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel exhausting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That part I have noticed, pretty strongly.\n\nIt’s not just being tired at the end of the night, because I’m used to being tired after a shift. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. By the time I carry a couple cases of liquor or set up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double, even if I barely did anything earlier that day.\n\nAnd those aren’t even huge tasks, you know? They’re things I’ve done a thousand times without thinking. Stocking bottles, cutting fruit, dragging a trash bag out, wiping everything down before we open. Lately it feels like every step has a weight tied to it. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly. I keep telling myself, “Come on, just do the next thing,” but even small stuff feels like I have to talk myself into it. Not because I don’t know how to do it, just because there’s no energy there. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{
  "candidates": ["F32.2", "F32.3", "F33.2", "F33.3"],
  "note": "The patient continues to meet criteria for moderate or severe depressive episodes, either as part of a single episode (F32.2, F32.3) or recurrent episodes (F33.2, F33.3). No new disorders are added, and no candidates are ruled out definitively at this time.",
  "is_final": false
}

It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel exhausting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That part I have noticed, pretty strongly.\n\nIt’s not just being tired at the end of the night, because I’m used to being tired after a shift. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. By the time I carry a couple cases of liquor or set up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double, even if I barely did anything earlier that day.\n\nAnd those aren’t even huge tasks, you know? They’re things I’ve done a thousand times without thinking. Stocking bottles, cutting fruit, dragging a trash bag out, wiping everything down before we open. Lately it feels like every step has a weight tied to it. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly. I keep telling myself, “Come on, just do the next thing,” but even small stuff feels like I have to talk myself into it. Not because I don’t know how to do it, just because there’s no energy there. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long have you been feeling this way? Have these symptoms been going on for at least two weeks straight, or have they come and gone during that time?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 51 / bartender / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about two months, since the bar was bought out and everything I had been working toward got changed.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s been… about two months now, I think. I’m trying to place it because I didn’t wake up one morning and go, “Okay, now I’m like this.” It kind of settled in after the bar got bought out.

That’s when everything I had been working toward there got changed around, and after that it’s like I just never really got back to feeling normal. So yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. It hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I was fine. There are maybe hours or parts of a day where I’m distracted enough that I’m not thinking about it as much, but overall it’s been pretty steady for those two months. It’s just been hanging around.

Like, the things I used to care about, the things that made me feel like I was me—I just don’t feel anything for them now.\n\nI used to get excited about murals, you know, seeing them around town or thinking about colors and lines and what someone was trying to say with them. I used to run, and even if I wasn’t fast or anything, there was a feeling to it. Basketball too—watching it, playing a little, talking about it. And even at work, making a really good drink, getting it just right, that used to give me some pride.\n\nNow I just kind of stand there. I look at the same stuff and it’s like… nothing. Like I’m watching someone else’s life from across the room. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in those activities completely, or if you still want to do them but just can’t seem to feel anything from them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like… I don’t even really want to do them anymore, not in the way I used to. Or maybe I go through the motion of wanting to, because I remember that I used to be that person. Like with murals, I’ll see one and I know, in my head, that normally I’d stop and look at the details, the colors, the way the lines are done, all of that. I used to get this little spark from it. Now I just look at it and it’s flat. Like it has nothing to do with me.\n\nSame with running or basketball. It’s not even that I’m sitting there wishing I could enjoy it and then failing. It’s more like the wanting part got shut off too. And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. It doesn’t land anywhere.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is both, but mostly I’ve lost interest. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or depressed most of the time, even outside of not feeling interested in those activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. Like… I don’t walk around thinking, “I’m sad,” exactly. It’s not always that clear. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.\n\nBut if you mean, do I feel low even when I’m not specifically thinking about murals or running or work or whatever… yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worse about yourself than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.\n\nI’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.\n\nSo I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. If it’s happening, I’m not very aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live, or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. manifestation: Inventory used to be automatic for me, but now I stare at the bottles and lose count three times, or I freeze trying to decide something simple like what garnish to restock.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before I sank into this after the new owners took over, I never believed I had ruined the place or that people were being punished because of me. Those thoughts only started once I got depressed these past couple months.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I've missed several shifts and made closing mistakes I never used to make, and now my coworkers have to double-check work that people used to trust me with. I also stopped seeing friends for runs, basketball, or mural shoots because I can't make myself show up.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since the bar was bought out and everything I had been working toward got changed.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 51 / bartender / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S025\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live, or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I have some clear thing to tell you, like a plan or anything I’ve decided. It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I haven’t really sat there and made a decision about it. It’s more like these thoughts pass through, and I try not to look straight at them too long.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel exhausting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have these symptoms been going on for at least two weeks straight, or have they come and gone during that time?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about two months now, I think. So yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. It hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I was fine. There are maybe hours or parts of a day where I’m distracted enough that I’m not thinking about it as much, but overall it’s been pretty steady for those two months. It’s just been hanging around.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had periods like this before, where you felt this way for weeks at a time, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know… I’m trying to think back and be fair about it.

I’ve had rough patches before, sure. I mean, I’m 51, I’ve had jobs go sideways, relationships be stressful, money be tight, all the normal life stuff. There have been times I was down or worn out for a while, but I don’t know if it was like this, exactly. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, that was the same thing for weeks.”

Maybe there were stretches where I was in a bad mood or felt beat up by life, but this feels harder to shake. Or maybe I just didn’t pay attention before and kept working through it. I’m not sure. I don’t want to say something definite if I can’t really remember it clearly.

So… I guess I’d say I’ve had bad periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had one quite like this, going on this long and feeling this heavy.

I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in those activities completely, or if you still want to do them but just can’t seem to feel anything from them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like… I don’t even really want to do them anymore, not in the way I used to. Or maybe I go through the motion of wanting to, because I remember that I used to be that person. Like with murals, I’ll see one and I know, in my head, that normally I’d stop and look at the details, the colors, the way the lines are done, all of that. I used to get this little spark from it. Now I just look at it and it’s flat. Like it has nothing to do with me.\n\nSame with running or basketball. It’s not even that I’m sitting there wishing I could enjoy it and then failing. It’s more like the wanting part got shut off too. And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or depressed most of the time, even outside of not feeling interested in those activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.\n\nBut if you mean, do I feel low even when I’m not specifically thinking about murals or running or work or whatever… yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worse about yourself than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.\n\nI’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.\n\nSo I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.\n\nI mean, at work there are moments where I feel like I’m dragging, like everything takes more effort than it should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.\n\nI mean, at work there are moments where I feel like I’m dragging, like everything takes more effort than it should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. If it’s happening, I’m not very aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live, or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I have some clear thing to tell you, like a plan or anything I’ve decided. It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I haven’t really sat there and made a decision about it. It’s more like these thoughts pass through, and I try not to look straight at them too long.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel exhausting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That part I have noticed, pretty strongly.\n\nIt’s not just being tired at the end of the night, because I’m used to being tired after a shift. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. By the time I carry a couple cases of liquor or set up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double, even if I barely did anything earlier that day.\n\nAnd those aren’t even huge tasks, you know? They’re things I’ve done a thousand times without thinking. Stocking bottles, cutting fruit, dragging a trash bag out, wiping everything down before we open. Lately it feels like every step has a weight tied to it. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly. I keep telling myself, “Come on, just do the next thing,” but even small stuff feels like I have to talk myself into it. Not because I don’t know how to do it, just because there’s no energy there. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have these symptoms been going on for at least two weeks straight, or have they come and gone during that time?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about two months now, I think. I’m trying to place it because I didn’t wake up one morning and go, “Okay, now I’m like this.” It kind of settled in after the bar got bought out.\n\nThat’s when everything I had been working toward there got changed around, and after that it’s like I just never really got back to feeling normal. So yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. It hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I was fine. There are maybe hours or parts of a day where I’m distracted enough that I’m not thinking about it as much, but overall it’s been pretty steady for those two months. It’s just been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before, where you felt this way for weeks at a time, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think back and be fair about it.\n\nI’ve had rough patches before, sure. I mean, I’m 51, I’ve had jobs go sideways, relationships be stressful, money be tight, all the normal life stuff. There have been times I was down or worn out for a while, but I don’t know if it was like this, exactly. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, that was the same thing for weeks.”\n\nMaybe there were stretches where I was in a bad mood or felt beat up by life, but this feels harder to shake. Or maybe I just didn’t pay attention before and kept working through it. I’m not sure. I don’t want to say something definite if I can’t really remember it clearly.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’ve had bad periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had one quite like this, going on this long and feeling this heavy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not really sure, honestly.

I’ve had times where I was in a slump, or where life felt pretty flat for a while, but when you say “at least two weeks” and “down most days” and also losing interest in things I usually care about… I don’t know if I can confidently say yes to that from before.

Like, I’ve gone through stretches where work was stressful or I didn’t feel like going out much, but I don’t know if that was the same thing or if it was just being worn down by whatever was happening at the time. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.2, F32.3, F33.2, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess I came in because… nothing really feels like mine anymore, if that makes sense. Like, the things I used to care about, the things that made me feel like I was me—I just don’t feel anything for them now.\n\nI used to get excited about murals, you know, seeing them around town or thinking about colors and lines and what someone was trying to say with them. I used to run, and even if I wasn’t fast or anything, there was a feeling to it. Basketball too—watching it, playing a little, talking about it. And even at work, making a really good drink, getting it just right, that used to give me some pride.\n\nNow I just kind of stand there. I look at the same stuff and it’s like… nothing. Like I’m watching someone else’s life from across the room. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in those activities completely, or if you still want to do them but just can’t seem to feel anything from them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like… I don’t even really want to do them anymore, not in the way I used to. Or maybe I go through the motion of wanting to, because I remember that I used to be that person. Like with murals, I’ll see one and I know, in my head, that normally I’d stop and look at the details, the colors, the way the lines are done, all of that. I used to get this little spark from it. Now I just look at it and it’s flat. Like it has nothing to do with me.\n\nSame with running or basketball. It’s not even that I’m sitting there wishing I could enjoy it and then failing. It’s more like the wanting part got shut off too. And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. It doesn’t land anywhere.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is both, but mostly I’ve lost interest. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or depressed most of the time, even outside of not feeling interested in those activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. Like… I don’t walk around thinking, “I’m sad,” exactly. It’s not always that clear. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.\n\nBut if you mean, do I feel low even when I’m not specifically thinking about murals or running or work or whatever… yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worse about yourself than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.\n\nI’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.\n\nSo I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.\n\nI mean, at work there are moments where I feel like I’m dragging, like everything takes more effort than it should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. If it’s happening, I’m not very aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live, or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I have some clear thing to tell you, like a plan or anything I’ve decided. It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I haven’t really sat there and made a decision about it. It’s more like these thoughts pass through, and I try not to look straight at them too long.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel exhausting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That part I have noticed, pretty strongly.\n\nIt’s not just being tired at the end of the night, because I’m used to being tired after a shift. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. By the time I carry a couple cases of liquor or set up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double, even if I barely did anything earlier that day.\n\nAnd those aren’t even huge tasks, you know? They’re things I’ve done a thousand times without thinking. Stocking bottles, cutting fruit, dragging a trash bag out, wiping everything down before we open. Lately it feels like every step has a weight tied to it. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly. I keep telling myself, “Come on, just do the next thing,” but even small stuff feels like I have to talk myself into it. Not because I don’t know how to do it, just because there’s no energy there. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Have these symptoms been going on for at least two weeks straight, or have they come and gone during that time?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about two months now, I think. I’m trying to place it because I didn’t wake up one morning and go, “Okay, now I’m like this.” It kind of settled in after the bar got bought out.\n\nThat’s when everything I had been working toward there got changed around, and after that it’s like I just never really got back to feeling normal. So yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. It hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I was fine. There are maybe hours or parts of a day where I’m distracted enough that I’m not thinking about it as much, but overall it’s been pretty steady for those two months. It’s just been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before, where you felt this way for weeks at a time, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think back and be fair about it.\n\nI’ve had rough patches before, sure. I mean, I’m 51, I’ve had jobs go sideways, relationships be stressful, money be tight, all the normal life stuff. There have been times I was down or worn out for a while, but I don’t know if it was like this, exactly. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, that was the same thing for weeks.”\n\nMaybe there were stretches where I was in a bad mood or felt beat up by life, but this feels harder to shake. Or maybe I just didn’t pay attention before and kept working through it. I’m not sure. It’s just been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before, where you felt this way for weeks at a time, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think back and be fair about it.\n\nI’ve had rough patches before, sure. I mean, I’m 51, I’ve had jobs go sideways, relationships be stressful, money be tight, all the normal life stuff. There have been times I was down or worn out for a while, but I don’t know if it was like this, exactly. I can’t really point to another time and say, “Yes, that was the same thing for weeks.”\n\nMaybe there were stretches where I was in a bad mood or felt beat up by life, but this feels harder to shake. Or maybe I just didn’t pay attention before and kept working through it. I’m not sure. I don’t want to say something definite if I can’t really remember it clearly.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I’ve had bad periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had one quite like this, going on this long and feeling this heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one—lasting at least two weeks—where your mood was down most days and you lost interest in things you usually care about?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly.\n\nI’ve had times where I was in a slump, or where life felt pretty flat for a while, but when you say “at least two weeks” and “down most days” and also losing interest in things I usually care about… I don’t know if I can confidently say yes to that from before.\n\nThere were periods where I was unhappy, sure. Like, I’ve gone through stretches where work was stressful or I didn’t feel like going out much, but I don’t know if that was the same thing or if it was just being worn down by whatever was happening at the time. I guess I never really tracked it that closely. I’d just keep working, go home, sleep, repeat, and not think of it as some clear episode.\n\nSo I don’t want to say no like it’s impossible, but I also can’t point to a specific time before this where I know I felt that way for two weeks or more and stopped caring about things. I just… I’m not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you recall if you’ve ever had a time before this one—lasting at least two weeks—where you felt down most days and stopped caring about things you usually enjoy, even if it wasn’t exactly the same as what you’re feeling now?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 51 / bartender / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m trying to pull one out, and I just… I can’t really land on one clearly.

I mean, if you asked me, “Have you ever been unhappy for a couple weeks?” I’d probably say yes, because life has had plenty of stretches like that. But when you put it together that way — down most days, for at least two weeks, and not caring about the things I usually enjoy — I don’t know. I don’t have a clean memory of that happening before.

There were times where I didn’t go out much, or I’d just work and come home and not do a whole lot, but I don’t know if that was because I’d stopped caring or because I was tired, or busy, or just in a routine. I’ve always been the kind of person who just keeps moving and doesn’t sit there measuring how bad it is, so looking back, it’s kind of blurry.

So… I can’t honestly say yes. It might’ve happened in some milder way, but I don’t remember a definite period like this before.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess I came in because… nothing really feels like mine anymore, if that makes sense. Like, the things I used to care about, the things that made me feel like I was me—I just don’t feel anything for them now.\n\nI used to get excited about murals, you know, seeing them around town or thinking about colors and lines and what someone was trying to say with them. I used to run, and even if I wasn’t fast or anything, there was a feeling to it. Basketball too—watching it, playing a little, talking about it. And even at work, making a really good drink, getting it just right, that used to give me some pride.\n\nNow I just kind of stand there. I look at the same stuff and it’s like… nothing. Like I’m watching someone else’s life from across the room. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve lost interest in those activities completely, or if you still want to do them but just can’t seem to feel anything from them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more like… I don’t even really want to do them anymore, not in the way I used to. Or maybe I go through the motion of wanting to, because I remember that I used to be that person. Like with murals, I’ll see one and I know, in my head, that normally I’d stop and look at the details, the colors, the way the lines are done, all of that. I used to get this little spark from it. Now I just look at it and it’s flat. Like it has nothing to do with me.\n\nSame with running or basketball. It’s not even that I’m sitting there wishing I could enjoy it and then failing. It’s more like the wanting part got shut off too. And at work, making a good drink… I know when I’ve done it right, I know what it’s supposed to feel like to take pride in that, but I just kind of stand there and do it. It doesn’t land anywhere.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is both, but mostly I’ve lost interest. I don’t care about any of it the way I used to, and that’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or depressed most of the time, even outside of not feeling interested in those activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those things, honestly. Like… I don’t walk around thinking, “I’m sad,” exactly. It’s not always that clear. A lot of the time it’s more like everything is heavy or muted, like the color got turned down on the whole day.\n\nBut if you mean, do I feel low even when I’m not specifically thinking about murals or running or work or whatever… yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it depressed every minute, because sometimes I’m just kind of blank, or I’m distracted by whatever I’m doing. But underneath it, there’s this dragged-down feeling that seems to be there most of the time.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because I keep second-guessing myself. Like maybe I’m just tired, or maybe I’m just in a bad stretch. But it doesn’t really lift the way a normal bad mood would. It’s just there, kind of sitting on everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite lately, or do you find yourself feeling worse about yourself than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep, definitely. I mean, appetite I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but sleep has been bad.\n\nI’ll be exhausted by the time I lie down, like my body feels completely done, especially after work. But as soon as I’m in bed, my head just starts going. It’s like it picks up every little thing I did wrong that day and plays it back at me. If I messed up an order, or took too long with someone’s drink, or said something awkward to a customer, or even just looked like I didn’t know what I was doing… it all comes back. And it doesn’t feel like normal thinking, exactly. It feels like I’m stuck reviewing evidence against myself.\n\nSo I’m lying there tired, but I can’t drop off because my mind won’t shut up. Then when I finally do sleep, it’s not like I sleep through. I’ve been waking up around four or five in the morning, and once I’m awake, that’s usually it. I just lie there and try to force myself back down, but it doesn’t happen. Then I’m just waiting for the day to start, already feeling worn out before anything’s even happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your appetite has changed lately—like if you're eating more or less than usual—or if you've noticed any recent weight loss or gain without trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’ve been eating less. Not in some planned way, not like I’m trying to lose weight or anything. It’s more like food just doesn’t occur to me the way it used to.\n\nMost days I’ll get through a big part of the shift and realize I haven’t eaten because my hands start shaking behind the bar. Like I’ll be reaching for something or trying to pour and I notice that trembly feeling, and then it hits me that I’ve had maybe coffee or nothing, and it’s already way too late in the day. But even then it’s not like I suddenly feel hungry in a normal way. It’s more like, “Oh, I guess I should put something in my body so I don’t fall over.”\n\nSometimes after closing I’ll grab whatever’s around, like a few bites of something cold, something that’s been sitting there, and I’ll just kind of force it down. And then I feel sick looking at it, or sick thinking about eating more. It’s strange because I know I need to eat, but the idea of food feels… I don’t know, pointless or kind of gross.\n\nAs far as weight, I don’t know exactly. I haven’t really been checking. My clothes maybe feel a little different, but I couldn’t tell you a number. It’s more the eating itself that’s changed. I just forget, and then when I remember, I don’t want it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or unable to sit still, especially during the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed it in a clear way, like I don’t think I’m pacing around the room all day or anything like that. And I don’t know that I’d say I’m moving dramatically slower either.\n\nI mean, at work there are moments where I feel like I’m dragging, like everything takes more effort than it should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking. It’s not like someone has said, “Why are you moving so slow?” or “Sit still,” at least not that I can think of. Sometimes I’m just standing there and it takes me a second to get going, but that might just be me being tired or distracted.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed either one in a definite way. If it’s happening, I’m not very aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live, or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I have some clear thing to tell you, like a plan or anything I’ve decided. It’s more… sometimes everything feels so heavy that I catch myself thinking, “What is the point of doing this again tomorrow?” But then I don’t know if that’s the same as not wanting to live.\n\nI guess I’ve had moments where life feels kind of not worth much, or like I’m just taking up space and making things harder, but it’s hard for me to sort out what’s a real thought and what’s just me being exhausted and worn down. I haven’t really sat there and made a decision about it. It’s more like these thoughts pass through, and I try not to look straight at them too long.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’m actively trying to do anything to myself, but yeah, I guess there are times it feels like it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have significantly less energy than usual, or that even small tasks feel exhausting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That part I have noticed, pretty strongly.\n\nIt’s not just being tired at the end of the night, because I’m used to being tired after a shift. I’ve been bartending long enough to know what normal tired feels like. This is different. It’s like I start out already behind somehow. By the time I carry a couple cases of liquor or set up the well, my body feels like I’ve already worked a double, even if I barely did anything earlier that day.\n\nAnd those aren’t even huge tasks, you know? They’re things I’ve done a thousand times without thinking. Stocking bottles, cutting fruit, dragging a trash bag out, wiping everything down before we open. Lately it feels like every step has a weight tied to it. I’ll do one thing and then have to stand there for a second, not because it was impossible, but because I feel drained in this weird, deep way.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly. I keep telling myself, “Come on, just do the next thing,” but even small stuff feels like I have to talk myself into it. Not because I don’t know how to do it, just because there’s no energy there. Like the tank is empty before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? So yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. It hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I was fine. I’m trying to place it because I didn’t wake up one morning and go, “Okay, now I’m like this.” It kind of settled in after the bar got bought out.\n\nThat’s when everything I had been working toward there got changed around, and after that it’s like I just never really got back to feeling normal. So yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. It hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I was fine. There are maybe hours or parts of a day where I’m distracted enough that I’m not thinking about it as much, but overall it’s been pretty steady for those two months. It’s just been hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before, where you felt this way for weeks at a time, or is this the first time you’ve experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think back and be fair about it.\n\nI’ve had rough patches before, sure. I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, and I’m not trying to dodge it. I just genuinely don’t have a clean answer.

When I think back, I can remember being tired of things, or not wanting to deal with people, or just getting through work and going home and not doing much. But whether that was me being down most days and actually losing interest in things I cared about for two straight weeks… I don’t know. I didn’t really keep track like that, and at the time I don’t think I would’ve described it that way.

So if I’m being honest, I can’t recall a definite time before this where I’d say yes, that happened for at least two weeks in a row. Maybe there were stretches that looked similar from the outside, but in my memory it’s blurry. This current one stands out more to me than anything I can remember before.

Like, the things I used to care about, the things that made me feel like I was me—I just don’t feel anything for them now.\n\nI used to get excited about murals, you know, seeing them around town or thinking about colors and lines and what someone was trying to say with them.
